### DSN-PAGES-16 [low] Sending the contact form drops keyboard focus to <body>: the submit button is disabled while sending and nothing puts focus back
src/components/public/contact/ContactForm.tsx:226 fix:trivial conf:high prior:none found
EVIDENCE: <ActionButton type="submit" disabled={!scripted || !turnstileAvailable || status.kind === 'sending'}> (line 226) disables the focused button for the request; on success the fields are cleared and on failure only a 422 moves focus to a field (lines 123-136). contact2.mjs (function answered inside the browser with page.route fulfil, no request left Playwright): after Enter on «أرسل», document.activeElement is BODY for the success, a 502, a 429 and a network failure; only the INVALID reply focuses #contact-email.
IMPACT: A keyboard or screen-reader visitor loses their place after sending (or after a failed send they want to retry): the next Tab or virtual-cursor move starts from the page top in many screen readers, and a failed send gives them no focused control to press again.
FIX: Keep the button focusable while sending (aria-disabled="true" plus the existing status.kind === 'sending' guard in submit) or, after the reply, focus the status line or the button again.

### DSN-PAGES-17 [low] Stepping through the lightbox announces only «2 / 19» (read as a slash, in Latin order), never the caption of the photo that appeared
src/components/public/scenes/SceneGallery.tsx:173 fix:trivial conf:medium prior:none found
EVIDENCE: The only live region in the dialog is <span dir="ltr" aria-live="polite" className="t-label">{index + 1} / {shown.length}</span> (SceneGallery.tsx:173-175); the caption is a plain <span className={styles.caption}> in the footer (line 201) and the new <img alt={current.caption}> is not announced when it replaces the old one. scenes.mjs: ArrowLeft changes the live text from «2 / 19» to «3 / 19»; focus stays on «إغلاق» or a step button, so nothing else is read.
IMPACT: A screen-reader visitor stepping through Anas's photos hears «ثلاثة شرطة مائلة تسعة عشر» for each one and has to leave the step buttons to find out what the photo is; sighted visitors get the caption at once.
FIX: Make the announcement carry the caption in Arabic order, e.g. a visually hidden live span «{caption}، الصورة {n} من {total}», and drop aria-live from the visual counter.

### DSN-PAGES-18 [low] On /book only the characters section disappears when its list is empty: an empty excerpts, photos or editions list leaves a bare heading (and its contents link) on the page
src/components/public/book/BookView.tsx:120 fix:trivial conf:high prior:none found
EVIDENCE: bookRoomFields (src/admin/collections/rooms.ts:273-287) makes excerpts, characters, photos and editions plain lists with no minimum (fields.ts:337-340: z.array, no min), so the admin can publish any of them empty. BookView drops the characters section and its SectionNav entry when that list is empty (BookView.tsx:52, 143), but always renders «اقتباسات» (lines 120-141), «صور» (181-201) and the editions (233-260), and SECTIONS keeps their links. book-empty.mjs (the /book markup with the excerpt figures and photos removed, as BookView renders empty lists): «اقتباسات» over a lone 1px rule, 146px; «صور» with nothing under it, 155px; the contents strip still lists both (shots/book-empty-excerpts-360.png).
IMPACT: If Anas takes the quotations down while the final manuscript (E04) changes, or clears the photos, the book page shows headings over nothing and a contents link to an empty section, the placeholder look DESIGN.md section 8 rules out («A section waiting for Anas's material is left out»).
FIX: Treat every list the way characters are treated: filter SECTIONS by which lists have items and render the excerpts, photos and editions sections only when non-empty (one helper, e.g. const has = { excerpts: book.excerpts.length > 0, ... }).

### PERF-02 [low] Every film poster is downloaded at page start, at its single full size, even far below the fold: 278 KB on /started and 239 KB on /built of a phone's first load
src/components/weave/VideoTile.tsx:87 fix:small conf:high prior:lead:PERF-04 (same defect; its A/B saw LCP unchanged on /started and 244 ms on /built, so the byte saving is the firm part).
EVIDENCE: VideoTile.tsx:87 `poster={`/${entry.poster}`}` on a `<video preload="none">`: a poster has no lazy loading, and images.ts:43-46 gives a poster a single size (`srcSet: /${file} ${width}w`). Measured (runs/mob/started.json, 360x800, Slow 4G): the 7 posters (17.8, 21.6, 70.4, 22.4, 87.5, 25.2, 33.9 KB = 278 KB) are requested at 759-776 ms with Medium priority, before the body font (requested 1881 ms) and in parallel with the CSS and the 134 KB of JS; all 7 videos report inView=false at 360. /built: 5 posters, 239 KB, requested at 826-839 ms (3 of 5 out of view). /passed: 2 posters, 71 KB, both out of view. A/B with the posters blocked (scratch ab.mjs, 3 runs each, a loaded machine so the spread is wide): FCP 3632/3912/3704 -> 2364/2532/5104 ms and LCP 6016/6552/7028 -> 2364/2532/9052 ms with the document on the throttled network; with the document served through the same route in both arms, FCP 4664/3148/3256 -> 3360/2596/3068 ms and LCP 6408/4380/4412 -> 5020/4012/4288 ms. The byte saving is deterministic: 278 KB of the page's 811 KB first load on /started at 360.
IMPACT: On a slow phone connection about 1.5 s of bandwidth (278 KB at 180 KB/s) goes to pictures the visitor has not scrolled to, competing with the fonts, the scripts and the first-screen content of the three film rooms; it also spends metered mobile data on films the visitor may never play.
FIX: Render the poster as an <img loading="lazy" decoding="async"> (with width/height) inside the tile, hidden once the film plays, and drop the poster attribute (or set it from the IntersectionObserver that already exists in motion.ts); optionally give posters 360/720 derivatives in prepare-media so the img can carry a srcset.

### PERF-03 [low] Every public page downloads the whole application's CSS as one render-blocking file: the admin, the book reader, the store and every room (76 KB raw, 12.6 KB gzip)
next.config.ts:36 RUNTIME fix:trivial conf:medium
EVIDENCE: All 26 public HTML files and all admin pages link /_next/static/chunks/37lmkiv9a5z5_.css (75,985 B, 12,627 B gzip), in both the 01:30 and the 15:30 build. Split by CSS module (scratch cssbreak.mjs): reader 13,751 B, store 11,621 B, admin 7,506 B, journal 4,740 B, scenes 4,732 B, site 4,273 B, book 4,221 B, shelf 3,278 B, home 2,817 B, contact 2,674 B ... A policy page (policies/[slug]/page.tsx uses store.module.css for its shell) needs none of the reader, admin, journal, scenes, book, shelf, home, contact, built, passed, rooms, figure, story or roomnav rules: 50,121 B of the 75,985. next.config.ts sets no `experimental.cssChunking`, so Turbopack uses the default `true` (merge whenever possible); node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/05-config/01-next-config-js/cssChunking.md documents the Turbopack-only `'graph'` strategy with `requestCost` (default 20000 bytes) to stop routes downloading CSS they never import.
IMPACT: A first visit to any page blocks rendering on about 50 KB of CSS it does not use (the admin and reader styles included); small on its own (roughly 8 KB gzip, ~50 ms at Slow 4G plus parse), but it sits on the critical path of every page.
FIX: Set experimental: { globalNotFound: true, cssChunking: { type: 'graph', requestCost: 5000 } } in next.config.ts, rebuild, and keep it only if the per-page CSS bytes and request count measured on out/ improve.
PROOF: pnpm build; for each out/*.html sum the gzip of its <link rel=stylesheet> files before and after; expect policies/*.html and store.html to drop from ~15 KB to ~5 KB gzip with at most one or two extra CSS requests.

### PERF-06 [low] check:budgets enforces only the JavaScript budget; the plan's second public budget, initial HTML+CSS+fonts+hero under 1 MiB, is checked nowhere
scripts/check-budgets.mjs:22 fix:small conf:high prior:I49 (5) and I50 (2) record the JS headroom only; wave B TEST-E2E-16 covers the pages CI never builds, not this rule.
EVIDENCE: VERIFICATION.md:55 retains two public budgets: "Initial HTML+CSS+fonts+hero <1MiB, public initial hydrated JS <150KiB gzip"; VERIFICATION.md:57 names `pnpm check:budgets` as the budget proof. check-budgets.mjs defines one constant (line 22, PUBLIC_JS_GZIP_BUDGET_BYTES) and sums only `<script src>` chunks (54-77); `grep -rn '1 MiB\|1048576\|1024 \* 1024' scripts tests .github` finds no other budget. Measured today (runs/mob, transferred): HTML+CSS+fonts+LCP image = 251-546 KB per page, largest /book 546 KB (fonts 391 KB, hero 133 KB): it passes, unguarded.
IMPACT: A heavier hero, a sixth font face or a larger CSS chunk can push a page past the plan's 1 MiB first-load budget with every check green; P10 would discover it only if someone measures by hand.
FIX: Extend check-budgets.mjs: per public page, add the gzip of the HTML and of its linked stylesheets, the bytes of the fonts the CSS declares (all five @font-face files is a safe upper bound), and the largest srcset candidate of any <img>/<source> marked fetchpriority=high or loading=eager, and fail above 1 MiB.

### PERF-07 [low] check:budgets counts only first-party chunks: the Turnstile script that /contact, /orders and the checkout load at hydration (27.6 KB gzip) is never counted, so the checkout's real initial JS is about 176 KiB against the 150 KiB goal
scripts/check-budgets.mjs:68 fix:small conf:high prior:lead:PERF-01 (same defect, same numbers within 0.4 KB).
EVIDENCE: check-budgets.mjs:54 reads only `<script src>` tags and :68 `if (!src.startsWith('/_next/static/')) continue`. turnstile.ts:32-35 injects https://challenges.cloudflare.com/turnstile/v0/api.js from the ref callback of the widget box, i.e. on mount: ContactForm.tsx:235 in today's working tree (217 at HEAD; always), OrderPage.tsx:375 (the recovery form when the tab holds no order link), CheckoutForm.tsx:750 (as soon as the quote answers). Measured without any input: /contact requests it at 6065 ms at 360 and 292 ms at 1440, /orders at 4890 ms at 360 (runs/mob, runs/desk), /checkout 1669 ms after navigation with a mocked quote at 1440 (clsmock-ts.mjs). api.js today (one credential-free GET, 2026-10-07): a 302 to /turnstile/v0/g/4df4a60fa397/api.js, 86,732 B raw, 27,583 B gzip, 28,049 B br; its challenge frame loads more. First-party initial JS: checkout 148.9 KiB, orders 147.8, contact 141.0 (static.mjs). Cloudflare recommends executing the script "as early upon the visitor's page entry as possible" (https://developers.cloudflare.com/turnstile/get-started/client-side-rendering/, updated June 17, 2026), so loading it at hydration is deliberate; AvailabilityForm.tsx:176 arms it later as a trade-off.
IMPACT: The budget figure the plan relies on understates what the three form pages download by about 27 KiB, so the checkout already exceeds the 150 KiB goal in a browser while the check reports 148.9.
FIX: Make check-budgets add Turnstile's measured gzip size (a dated constant) to every page whose chunks contain `challenges.cloudflare.com/turnstile`, printed as its own line, and either count it against the 150 KiB goal and escalate the overrun as VERIFICATION.md:55 requires, or record in VERIFICATION that third-party scripts are budgeted separately.

### PERF-08 [low] prepare-media adds a source-width derivative for every source wider than 1800 px (2048, 2160, 2688), against its own comment; I49 (6) blames sources that grew wider instead
scripts/prepare-media.mjs:246 fix:trivial conf:high prior:I49 (6) records the effect with a wrong cause.
EVIDENCE: prepare-media.mjs:246 `if (widths.length === 0 || widths[widths.length - 1] < sourceWidth) widths.push(sourceWidth)`; the comment above (240-245) says it is for a source smaller than 360 or one in a gap between two breakpoints. For a 2048 px source, widths = [360,720,1200,1800] and 1800 < 2048, so 2048 is pushed. The committed manifest has recorded sourceWidth 2048 (13 vignettes), 2688 (02c-built-seven-lanterns, 90-divider) and 2160 (factory-yellow-mug_upscaled) with derivatives only up to 1800 since 70be5c9 (`git show 70be5c9:public/images/manifest.json`), so those sources did not grow: the next full run adds 16 derivatives from this rule alone. images.ts:53 puts every derivative into the srcset, so a 100vw or 1000px slot on a DPR 2 desktop then picks the 2048-2688 px file (the 1800 px files are already 528-673 KB). ISSUES.md I49 (6): "because some local sources are now wider than when the committed derivatives were made, it would add about 40 new image sizes".
IMPACT: The P10 media pass that I49 (6) schedules would silently grow the largest images on retina desktops by roughly a third, and the issue record points the fixer at the wrong cause.
FIX: Push the source width only when it is under the largest breakpoint: `if (widths.length === 0 || (widths.at(-1) < sourceWidth && sourceWidth < IMAGE_WIDTHS.at(-1))) widths.push(sourceWidth)`, and correct I49 (6).

### PERF-10 [low] public/_headers caches only /_next/static: fonts, images and the book PDF get Pages' default `max-age=0, must-revalidate`, so every full page load revalidates all five fonts before using them
public/_headers:52 RUNTIME fix:trivial conf:high prior:lead:PERF-02 (same defect).
EVIDENCE: public/_headers:51-53 in today's working tree (47-49 at HEAD 3a30a91; the working tree only added HSTS and Permissions-Policy to /*) sets `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` for /_next/static/* only; /fonts/*, /images/*, /brand/* and /book/* have no Cache-Control rule. Cloudflare Pages docs (https://developers.cloudflare.com/pages/configuration/serving-pages/, last updated Apr 21, 2026): Pages adds "Cache-Control: public, max-age=0, must-revalidate" and an ETag to cacheable assets. The font files never change in place (public/fonts/thmanyah/*.woff2 last touched 2026-09-23/27).
IMPACT: A returning visitor (a new tab, a link from a message, the next day) must wait one round trip per font and image before the browser may use its cached copy; on a mobile network the text shows in the fallback face and swaps again on every visit.
FIX: Add `/fonts/*` with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` (rename a font file if it ever changes) and `/images/*` and `/brand/*` with a moderate lifetime such as `public, max-age=86400, stale-while-revalidate=604800` (prepare-media rewrites derivatives under the same names).
PROOF: On the P10 Pages preview: `curl -sI https://<preview>.pages.dev/fonts/thmanyah/thmanyahsans-Regular.woff2 | grep -i cache-control` answers `public, max-age=0, must-revalidate` today; after the fix it must answer the new rule.

### PERF-11 [low] check:export accepts an export whose pages reference files that are not in it: the CI and Pages builds have none of the 18 films the rooms link to
scripts/check-export.mjs:100 fix:small conf:high prior:I33 records that a Pages build has no films; no check guards it. The earlier attempt filed this as a plan gap (lead plan gap 2).
EVIDENCE: check-export.mjs:100-102 checks only the REQUIRED list (pages, the PDF, _headers); check-budgets.mjs:70-74 checks only `<script src>` files. Nothing checks stylesheets, images, posters, fonts or `<source src>` films. public/media/*.mp4 is git-ignored (.gitignore:38 `/public/media/*`; `git ls-files public/media` lists only manifest.json), so the export CI builds (ci.yml:103-109) and a Pages build from the repository contain out/media/manifest.json and no film, while VideoTile.tsx:97 links `/media/<id>.mp4` on /started, /built and /passed; both checks pass on it.
IMPACT: A launch build without the films (I33 undecided, or a deploy from a fresh clone) goes out with green checks and every «تشغيل» answers «تعذّر تشغيل المقطع»; a missing CSS chunk or image derivative would pass the same way.
FIX: In check-export.mjs collect every local URL from the exported HTML (src, href of stylesheets and preloads, srcset/srcSet candidates, poster) and the CSS url()s, and fail on any file missing from out/; while I33 is open, allow /media/*.mp4 only for a loopback (demo-guard) build so a production build fails.

### PERF-12 [low] Opening the book on a phone costs about 0.8 MB and 10 s before the first page shows, and the plan's promised separate measurement of the lazy PDF chunks does not exist
scripts/check-budgets.mjs:56 fix:small conf:medium
EVIDENCE: VERIFICATION.md:55: "Lazy PDF/editor/board chunks measured separately." check-budgets.mjs:54-77 measures only the HTML's `<script src>`; nothing in scripts/ or tests/ measures the reader. Measured (scratch reader.mjs): clicking «افتح الكتاب» loads chunks 0v4iqqgqe7wb1.js 10.7 KB, 0mwmnd1geb7ar.js 7.2 KB, 11ph49ml3gu1-.js (pdf.js) 120 KB, 3y7oy_tt71u1t.js (page-flip) 10.3 KB, the worker out/_next/static/media/pdf.worker.min.3bl-ygmetel-a.mjs (1,265,413 B raw, 375,379 B gzip, 307,392 B br) and /book/khous-preview.pdf (364,653 B). First page drawn 753 ms after the click at 1440 unthrottled, 11,312 ms at 360/Slow 4G/4x (that run also fetched 234 KB of PDF ranges, which Pages would not serve).

FIX: Add a reader budget to check-budgets.mjs (the gzip of the chunks reachable from the PdfBookReader import plus the worker, failing above a recorded figure), and start the reader import and a low-priority fetch of the worker and PDF when the closed book scrolls into view (BookPreview.tsx), so the tap opens from cache.

### PERF-14 [low] Film posters exist in one size only, and /scenes uses four of them as photos: on a phone its preloaded first row downloads 147 KB of posters where 70 KB would do
scripts/prepare-media.mjs:291 fix:small conf:high
EVIDENCE: prepare-media.mjs:291 writes one poster per film (`resize({ width: 1200, withoutEnlargement: true })`), and images.ts:43-46 turns a poster into a single srcset candidate (`srcSet: /${file} ${width}w`). content/initial-content.json uses raha-coffee-roasting_HD-poster, raha-drone-branch_vertical-poster, raha-drone-opening_landscape-poster and raha-reel-community-poster as scenes. FIX: Have extractPoster write 360/720/1200 derivatives (never upscaled) and record them as `derivatives` in the manifest so imageSources builds a real srcset for posters, as it does for photos.

### QUALITY-01 [low] staff-admin's BAN_FAILED reply says the status changed, but the in-flight F1-9 code now puts the member back to active
supabase/functions/staff-admin/index.ts:146 fix:trivial conf:high prior:F1-9 / EF-ADMIN-16 (in flight, unaudited): the revert was added without the message
EVIDENCE: Line 137 (F1-9, uncommitted): `if (banError) await admin.from('staff').update({ active: true }).eq('user_id', userId)`; line 146 still answers `fail(500, 'BAN_FAILED', 'حُدّثت الحالة لكن تعذّر إيقاف الدخول. حاول مرة أخرى.')` ("the status was updated but blocking sign-in failed"). The revert's own result is not checked. TeamView.tsx:94 still says `BAN_FAILED commits active first`.
IMPACT: When the Auth admin API fails during a revoke, the owner reads that the status was updated, while the member is active again with full access (TeamView reloads and shows «نشط», contradicting the alert). If the revert update also fails, the member is left inactive and unbanned with no signal. Precondition: an Auth admin failure at revoke time.
FIX: Reword the BAN_FAILED message to say nothing changed (e.g. «تعذّر إيقاف الدخول، وبقي العضو نشطًا. حاول مرة أخرى.»), check the revert's `.error` and answer a distinct code when it fails, and update the TeamView.tsx:94 comment.

### QUALITY-02 [low] The refund actions report a provider refusal (a revoked or wrong key, 401/403) as a transient outage: «حاول بعد قليل»
supabase/functions/_shared/refunds.ts:116 fix:trivial conf:high prior:F1-11 / EF-MONEY-15 / P08-CKPT-05 (in flight) handle 'not_found' only
EVIDENCE: `const fetchFailed = (result: { kind: string }): Response => result.kind === 'not_found' ? fail(404, ...) : unavailable()`; `unavailable()` (line 113) is 503 PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE 'تعذّر الوصول إلى بوابة الدفع الآن. لم يتغيّر شيء؛ حاول بعد قليل.' A `refused` result (moyasar.ts:348, any 4xx but 404/429, and `refusedLocally()` for a non-UUID id) lands in the same 503.
IMPACT: refund-create, refund-recheck and refund-record-external tell the owner to retry shortly when Moyasar refused the key; retrying can never succeed until the secret is fixed, and nothing names the cause. The checkout side records CREATE_REFUSED_<status> and alerts (M1a-4); the refund side hides the same fault.
FIX: In fetchFailed, map kind 'refused' to its own reply, e.g. fail(502, 'PROVIDER_REFUSED', 'رفضت بوابة الدفع الطلب؛ تحقق من مفتاح الدفع في إعدادات الدوال.'), keeping 503 for 'unavailable' and 'rate_limited'.

### QUALITY-03 [low] The contact notice sanitizes the visitor's name with its own narrower rule instead of one(), so C0 line separators survive
supabase/functions/_shared/email.ts:250 fix:trivial conf:medium prior:EF-ADMIN-11 / F1-6 (in flight): EF-ADMIN-11's fix asked for one()'s cleanup (all Bidi_Control); F1-6 implemented the narrower VISITOR_BIDI, so the prior fix holds only partly
EVIDENCE: Contact notice: `const name = data.name.replace(VISITOR_BIDI, '').replace(/[\r\n\u0085  ]+/gu, ' ')` with `VISITOR_BIDI = /[‪-‮⁦-⁩]/gu` (line 232). The order-mail sanitizer in the same file, `one()` (line 346-352), replaces every `\p{Cc}`, `\p{Zl}`, `\p{Zp}` and drops every `\p{Bidi_Control}`. contact.ts:71 accepts any control character inside the name (`z.string().trim().min(1).max(120)`), and contacts.name has only a length CHECK.
IMPACT: A name containing U+000B, U+000C or U+001C-U+001E (mandatory line or paragraph breaks per UAX #14/#9) reaches the owner's notice, where the comment at line 249 says a line break 'would forge the lines below'; U+200E/U+200F/U+061C also survive. Limited to how the owner's mail client renders them.
FIX: Use the existing `one()` for the name and the email lines of renderContactNotice (it already isolates), and keep VISITOR_BIDI only for the multi-line message body.

### QUALITY-06 [low] CheckoutForm's manual quote refresh records a failure without the stale-run guard its sibling effect uses
src/components/store/CheckoutForm.tsx:279 fix:trivial conf:high
EVIDENCE: The debounced effect guards both branches: `.catch(() => { if (quoteRun.current !== run) return; setQuoteFailed(true) })` (227-230). refreshQuote (268-281) guards only success: `if (quoteRun.current === run) { setQuote(fresh) ... }` then `catch { setQuoteFailed(true) }` unguarded.
IMPACT: When a «أعد المحاولة» or POLICY_CHANGED refresh fails after a newer quote (from a cart, city or coupon change) already succeeded, the form shows «تعذّر تحديث الأسعار؛ أعد المحاولة بعد لحظات.» with a retry button beside a current quote. Cosmetic and self-healing, but it is the kind of drift two copies of one fetch produce.
FIX: Guard the catch the same way: `catch { if (quoteRun.current === run) setQuoteFailed(true) }` (or let refreshQuote and the effect share one fetch-and-apply helper).

### QUALITY-07 [low] The buyer's order page refuses a reply with any extra key, so an additive SQL change to order_summary or payment_view takes every order page down until the site is rebuilt
src/lib/orders.ts:305 fix:trivial conf:medium prior:lead:QUALITY-04 (confirmed)
EVIDENCE: orders.ts:223 `exact()` refuses unknown keys, and parseOrderView applies it to `order` (an exact 14-key list, line 305-320, built in SQL as `finance.order_summary(o.id) || {paidAt, refunded, testMode}`), `payment` (`payment_view(...) - 'orderId' - 'hasToken'`) and each item. The same order_summary feeds checkout_create, ACTIVE_HOLD and payment_attempt_begin replies, which quote.ts parseOrder reads leniently, and admin-orders.ts:13 states 'Extra keys are ignored'. fetchOrder (351-358) turns any parse failure into failure 'network'.
IMPACT: The functions and migrations deploy to Supabase separately from the Cloudflare Pages build. A migration that adds a field to finance.order_summary for the hold view (or to payment_view) makes every buyer's /orders page show «تعذّر الاتصال بالخدمة؛ أعد المحاولة.» until a rebuild with the new client lands; nothing in the checkout path warns, because its parser ignores the key.
FIX: Keep the strict checks on the keys the page reads but ignore unknown keys (drop the 'no other key' half of exact(), as the admin parsers do), or pin the order_summary shape in a contract test that feeds the real SQL reply through parseOrderView.

CheckoutForm now keeps `citiesState` 'loading' | 'failed' | 'loaded' with «تعذّر تحميل قائمة المدن.» and a retry, and «لا نوصل إلى أي مدينة حاليًا.» for an empty list (CheckoutForm.tsx:188-195, 690-710). CartView's select (318-336) has neither.
IMPACT: When /rest/v1/shipping_rates fails (or no city is enabled), a buyer of a physical item on /cart sees a city select holding only «اختر المدينة» while the quote says «اختر مدينة التوصيل.»: no reason, no retry. The checkout page recovers, so the buyer is confused rather than blocked.

### QUALITY-09 [low] Two of the admin's three step-up implementations read a failed factor lookup as 'no authenticator', and send an enrolled owner to enrol
src/components/admin/TeamView.tsx:84 fix:small conf:high prior:lead:QUALITY-09 (confirmed and extended); EF-ADMIN-19 is the dialog wording, not the callers
EVIDENCE: TeamView.tsx:84-89 `const { data } = await supabase.auth.mfa.listFactors(); const verified = data?.totp.find(...); if (!verified) { setNeedsEnrollment(true) ...`, and the same at CommerceSettingsForm.tsx:111-115. The third copy, RefundView.tsx:91-92 useStepUp, checks it: `const { data, error } = ...listFactors(); if (error) return 'failed'`, which the money screens show as «تعذّر الاتصال بالخادم.». listFactors makes a network call (it reads the user first), so it can fail on a lost connection. Also, CommerceSettingsForm.onStepUpVerified (133-142) feeds the retry's reply back into applyResult, which opens the dialog again on another STEP_UP_REQUIRED (109-117), against its header 'the same save retries exactly once' (lines 7-8); TeamView reports it instead.
IMPACT: On the team screen and the commerce settings (the invite, role change, revoke, seller details, policy approval and checkout switch), a transient failure after STEP_UP_REQUIRED shows «يلزم تفعيل تطبيق المصادقة أولًا» with a link to /admin/security to an owner who already has one: a wrong instruction on the screens that manage access and the store's switch.
FIX: Use RefundView's useStepUp/withStepUp in TeamView and CommerceSettingsForm (one implementation), or at least check `error` there and say «تعذّر الاتصال بالخادم.».

### QUALITY-10 [low] The outbox job records nothing when a database call fails mid-run, unlike the other two jobs, though its comment says the failure is recorded
supabase/functions/_shared/outbox.ts:317 fix:trivial conf:high
EVIDENCE: outbox.ts:227 `/** One dispatch run. Never throws: a failure is recorded as a failed run. */`, but `job_run_record` (line 311) sits inside the try and the catch (317-323) only sets `summary.status = 'failed'; return summary`. runMediaSweep (media-sweep.ts:67-75) and runPaymentsReconcile (payments.ts:774-792) call job_run_record after their try, so a failed run is recorded there. (Lines re-checked at submission; the file is being edited by the F1 round.)
IMPACT: When outbox_claim or outbox_result fails (a statement timeout, a grant or migration mistake) the run leaves no row; after 10 minutes the owner home says «بريد ينتظر الإرسال منذ أكثر من 10 دقائق. تأكد من الجدولة.» (AdminHome jobProblem/emailWaiting), pointing at the schedule, which works, instead of showing the job as «فاشل».
FIX: Move the job_run_record call after the try/catch (best effort, in its own try) as the other two jobs do, recording status 'failed' and a reason such as 'DB_FAILED'.

### QUALITY-11 [low] The three code checks (sign-in, step-up, enrolment) call every failure a wrong code, a lost connection and a rate limit included
src/components/admin/SignIn.tsx:62 fix:small conf:high prior:EF-ADMIN-19 (wave A) names StepUp and MfaEnroll; its fix F2b-7 changes StepUp only, so SignIn's code step and MfaEnroll keep the defect
EVIDENCE: SignIn.tsx:60-64 `const { error: verifyError } = await supabase.auth.verifyOtp(...); if (verifyError) { setError(BAD_CODE_MESSAGE)` («الرمز غير صحيح أو انتهت صلاحيته.»), while the same form's email step tells a request with no HTTP status apart (`if (sendError && !sendError.status) setMessage(OFFLINE_MESSAGE)`, line 43) and a 429 (line 47). StepUp.tsx:43-47 and MfaEnroll.tsx:74-81 answer «الرمز غير صحيح.» for any error of challengeAndVerify.
IMPACT: On a dropped connection or after the verification rate limit, Anas or a staff member is told the code was wrong, retypes it or asks for a new e-mail code (spending the send and verification limits) when waiting or reconnecting was the fix; in the step-up dialog the owner may conclude the authenticator is out of sync.
FIX: Mirror the email step in all three: no `status` → «تعذّر الاتصال. تحقق من الشبكة وحاول مرة أخرى.», 429 → the rate-limit sentence, else the wrong-code sentence (one small helper for the three).

### QUALITY-12 [low] CollectionForm still tracks the session's access token after D32 removed its only use, and hides the publish bar whenever it is null
src/components/admin/CollectionForm.tsx:362 fix:trivial conf:high prior:lead:QUALITY-14 (its token half, confirmed and rated low for the hidden publish bar)
EVIDENCE: CollectionForm.tsx:77 `const [accessToken, setAccessToken] = useState<string | null>(null)`, filled by getSession and an onAuthStateChange subscription (103-112), and read once: `{accessToken && baseSeq > 0 && (<PublishBar ...`. `git show eb41c2e -- src/components/admin/CollectionForm.tsx` (the D32 commit) removed `accessToken={accessToken}` from PublishBar; PublishBar takes no token today (its props, PublishBar.tsx:21-33).
IMPACT: A bearer token is copied into React state for a truthiness check, and an extra auth subscription runs per open document. When getSession resolves without a session (a refresh that failed offline) or an auth event carries none, the editor's نشر/جدولة/أرشفة bar silently disappears while the form and Save stay, with no sentence saying why.
FIX: Delete the accessToken state, its effect and the `accessToken &&` condition; the shell (AdminShell) already gates the admin on the session.

### QUALITY-13 [low] A book page's draw cannot be cancelled once its canvas is done, so a resize or a scroll-away during the text read leaves a stale text layer
src/components/book/pdf.ts:180 fix:trivial conf:medium
EVIDENCE: PageSurface.draw: `this.cancel(); this.drawn = key` (154-155), canvas render, then `this.text.replaceChildren()` (173), `const content = await page.getTextContent()` (180), `this.layer = new pdfjs.TextLayer({... container: this.text ...})` (191). cancel() (195-200) only cancels `this.task` (already null after the canvas) and `this.layer` (not yet set), and nothing re-checks `this.drawn` after line 180. pdf.js 6.3.289 TextLayer appends spans to the container as it processes items (build/pdf.mjs #processItems/#appendText).
IMPACT: StaticPdfReader redraws on every ResizeObserver tick and clears pages that scroll away; PdfBookReader redraws on size changes. A draw overtaken while awaiting getTextContent still appends its spans at the old scale (into a cleared host, or beside the new draw's layer when it lands after the new replaceChildren), so text selection and find-in-page on the /book preview can be misplaced or doubled until the next redraw. The visible canvas is unaffected.
FIX: Keep a draw generation (or compare `this.drawn === key`) after each await in draw() and return when superseded; in clear(), bump it too.

### QUALITY-14 [low] The restore rehearsal swallows a failed content-type read, uploads every object as octet-stream, and never checks the types it claims to restore
scripts/restore-check.mjs:236 fix:small conf:high prior:wave B plan gap 'A tool for the real restore's object upload' would reuse this uploadObjects code
EVIDENCE: bucketMimetypes: `try { return JSON.parse(result.stdout.trim()) } catch { return {} }` (233-237); uploadObjects then uses `contentType: types[item.name] || 'application/octet-stream'` (263). verifyObjects (345-372) compares only sha256 and counts. The header (lines 15-17) says objects 'are uploaded back with their original content types'.
IMPACT: A rehearsal can print OK while every restored object would carry application/octet-stream (paid-file keys have no extension, so nothing would correct it), and a real restore built from this procedure would ship images and PDFs with the wrong type. The rehearsal is the only proof D35 has that a backup restores correctly.
FIX: Let the parse failure throw (mustSucceed already stops on a failed psql), refuse an object with no recorded type instead of defaulting, and add a check that each restored object's metadata mimetype equals the backed-up one.

### QUALITY-15 [low] The daily media sweep records a bare «فاشل» and drops why: the paid-files tripwire and a Storage refusal look the same
supabase/functions/_shared/media-sweep.ts:58 fix:small conf:high prior:lead:QUALITY-01 (confirmed); ARCH-13 is about cron jobs that record no run at all
EVIDENCE: runMediaSweep: `} catch { summary.status = 'failed' }` around each sweep (lines 57-59, 63-65) and records `p_detail: { objects: summary.objects, tickets: summary.tickets, paidFiles: summary.paidFiles }` (line 70). sweepBucket throws `UNEXPECTED_KEY` when the candidate SQL lists a key outside the swept prefix and `REMOVE_FAILED` when Storage kept a key (lines 38, 41); neither code is kept or logged. AdminHome.tsx:381 renders the run as `${JOB_STATUS_LABEL[run.status]} (...)`. Also, `summary.objects` is assigned only after sweepBucket returns, so a run that removed 300 objects and then failed records 0.
IMPACT: If paid_files_sweep_candidates ever lists an `assets/` key (a SQL regression that would delete sold files), the guard refuses and the owner home shows «تنظيف الوسائط: فاشل», the same as a transient Storage error; which sweep failed and why is lost, so the one signal that the sweep's SQL went wrong cannot be told from noise.
FIX: Keep the code per sweep (`catch (error) { summary.status = 'failed'; summary.reason ??= `media:${(error as Error).message}` }`, 'paid:' for the second), add `reason` to p_detail as outbox.ts does, and let AdminHome show a distinct sentence for UNEXPECTED_KEY.

### QUALITY-16 [low] The new owner alerts lose their order number in alert_email_data, and the policy-republish alert has no text at all
supabase/migrations/20261002120000_order_emails.sql:216 fix:small conf:high prior:lead:QUALITY-02 (extended: F1-17 in flight now renders three kinds, but without the order number); M1a-3, M1a-4, M1b-7
EVIDENCE: alert_email_data computes v_number from orderId/attemptId/refundId but answers `if v_facts is null then return jsonb_build_object('alert', v_alert)` (line 216-217) for any kind its CASE does not list; the in-flight kinds attempt_mode_changed, refund_mode_changed, payment_create_refused (20261007100000_fable_audit_payments.sql:221, 613, 624) and policies_reset (20261007110000_fable_audit_operations.sql:1353) are not listed, and no later migration redefines the function. The F1 texts (email.ts:612-626, uncommitted) use `const order = alert.orderNumber ? one(alert.orderNumber) : UNKNOWN` (line 554), so they read «... بينما كانت دفعة الطلب غير معروف قيد التنفيذ ...». policies_reset has no case in alertText and falls to «وصل تنبيه جديد (policies_reset) يحتاج مراجعتك.» with the /admin/orders link (renderOwnerAlert).
IMPACT: When a payment or refund straddles a mode change, the owner's alert never names the order he must settle by hand; when a policy republish closes the open store, the owner gets a raw code and a link to the orders board instead of «the store just closed».
FIX: In the next SQL round add the four kinds to alert_email_data's CASE (an empty facts object is enough to keep orderNumber; `policy`/`seq` for policies_reset), add a policies_reset case to alertText with owner-approved Arabic, and give renderOwnerAlert a per-kind link (/admin/settings for policies_reset).

### QUALITY-17 [low] OrderView's act skips the re-read on a lost reply, unlike its copy in ReconciliationView, so a committed shipment shows as failed
src/components/admin/OrderView.tsx:181 fix:trivial conf:high prior:lead:QUALITY-05 (confirmed)
EVIDENCE: Doc (164-167): 'the order is read again whatever the answer'. Code: `const { data, error } = await call(); if (error) { text = error.code === '42501' ? NO_PERMISSION : SAVE_FAILED } else { unread = await reread(...); const reply = parseActionReply(data) ...}` (180-192), and a parse throw lands in `catch {}` with «تعذّر الحفظ» (193-195). ReconciliationView's act goes through run(), which re-reads in every outcome and answers an unreadable reply with BAD_REPLY (ReconciliationView.tsx:150-186). postgrest-js 2.117.1 answers a dropped connection with `{ error: { code: '' }, status: 0 }` and never retries a POST (dist/index.mjs:79-85, 416-455).
IMPACT: Operations mark items shipped on a flaky connection: the SQL commits and queues the shipped mail, the reply is lost, and the screen keeps «قيد التجهيز» with «تعذّر الحفظ. حاول مرة أخرى.»; the retry is refused as BAD_TRANSITION before a reload shows the truth. Two contradictory messages for one successful action; the SQL guards against harm.
FIX: Re-read in every outcome (move the reread after the try/catch, as moneyRun does) and answer a parse failure with BAD_REPLY; or route act through the same run() helper ReconciliationView uses.

### QUALITY-18 [low] The staff order search checks an address without the punycode step every server copy applies, so an Arabic-domain buyer cannot be found by his address
src/lib/admin-orders.ts:132 fix:small conf:high prior:lead:QUALITY-08 (confirmed)
EVIDENCE: normalizeOrderQuery: `const EMAIL = /^[a-z0-9._%+-]{1,64}@[a-z0-9.-]{1,190}\.(?:[a-z]{2,63}|xn--[a-z0-9-]{2,59})$/i` (120) and `EMAIL.test(query) ? query.toLowerCase() : null` (132). The server stores addresses after `toAsciiAddress` (contact.ts:53-68), applied by the email field written four times (contact.ts:72-79, checkout.ts:74-81, orders.ts:47-53, notify.ts:45-51); orders_list hashes the query as given (20261007110000_fable_audit_operations.sql:121-126).
IMPACT: A buyer whose address has an internationalised domain (the `.السعودية` case toAsciiAddress exists for) writes to Anas; operations paste his address into the orders search and get «أدخل رقم طلب كاملًا أو بريدًا إلكترونيًا.». The order is findable only by number or by typing the xn-- form.
FIX: Move EMAIL_SHAPE and toAsciiAddress into a pure _shared module (like saudi-mobile.ts) and run the search text through toAsciiAddress before the grammar check; let the four functions import the one email field from there.

### QUALITY-19 [low] After a successful store-settings action, a failed re-read keeps the old version, so the next action is refused as «another session»
src/components/admin/CommerceSettingsForm.tsx:105 fix:small conf:high prior:lead:QUALITY-10 (confirmed)
EVIDENCE: applyResult on success: `setSaved(successMessage); const loaded = await loadRow(); if (loaded) setRow(loaded); return` (103-107): a null re-read keeps `row.version` and says nothing. Every action sends `expectedVersion: row.version` (156, 240, 260); the admin function answers a stale version with 409 «تغيّرت الإعدادات من جلسة أخرى. أعد تحميل الصفحة.» (admin.ts commerceSettingsSave/commercePoliciesApprove/commerceCheckoutSet). The save and switch replies carry the new version (`ok({ version })`, `ok({ version, checkoutEnabled })`) and are discarded (`callFunction<{ version: number }>` result unused).
IMPACT: The owner saves the seller details, the re-read fails on a flaky connection, the screen says «تم الحفظ.»; his next press («اعتماد السياسات» or «افتح الشراء») is refused as a change from another session that never happened, and the store may open later than he believes.
FIX: Take the version from the action's own reply when it has one, and say REREAD_FAILED next to the success sentence when the re-read fails, as the order screens do.

### QUALITY-20 [low] The security page says the authenticator guards team actions only; it now also guards refunds, disputes, the seller details, the policies and the checkout switch
src/components/admin/MfaEnroll.tsx:105 fix:trivial conf:high prior:lead:QUALITY-12 (confirmed)
EVIDENCE: MfaEnroll.tsx:105 `مطلوب لتفعيل إجراءات الفريق: الدعوة وتغيير الدور والإيقاف.`, and its doc (18-21) says the same; StepUp.tsx:11-13 says the caller 'retries the original staff-admin call'. admin.ts handleAdmin answers STEP_UP_REQUIRED for refund-create, refund-record-external, dispute-record, commerce-settings-save, commerce-policies-approve, commerce-checkout-set and (F1, in flight) order-link-reissue with a new address (lines 236-267).
IMPACT: Anas, reading that the authenticator matters only for managing the team, may postpone enrolling it until he must refund a buyer or open the store, and is then stopped mid-action by «يلزم تفعيل تطبيق المصادقة أولًا».
FIX: Name what it guards (e.g. «مطلوب للإجراءات الحساسة: الاسترداد والنزاعات وبيانات البائع واعتماد السياسات وفتح الشراء وإدارة الفريق.») and correct the two comments.

### QUALITY-21 [low] The book reader handles a failed reader chunk and a failed PDF but not a failed page-flip chunk: an empty stage with dead controls
src/components/book/PdfBookReader.tsx:286 fix:trivial conf:high prior:lead:QUALITY-15 (confirmed); AUDIT-2 BOOK-4 covered the PDF load only
EVIDENCE: `void import('page-flip').then(({ PageFlip }) => { if (cancelled) return ... flip.loadFromHTML(...)` (286-310) has no rejection handler, so blockRef/flipRef are never set and turn/go return early. BookPreview catches the reader chunk (`import('./PdfBookReader').then(..., () => setState('failed'))`, BookPreview.tsx:30-33) and the PDF failure sets `failed` with «تعذّر فتح الصفحات.» and «إعادة المحاولة».
IMPACT: A visitor whose connection drops between the reader chunk and the page-flip chunk sees the reader's tools around an empty book with no message and no retry (the console logs an unhandled rejection); only «عرض للقراءة» or a reload recovers.
FIX: Add `.catch(() => { if (!cancelled) setFailed(true) })` to the page-flip import so the existing failure view and retry show.

### READER-03 [low] Going from two pages to one (rotating a tablet, narrowing the window) lands on the left-hand page of the spread and skips the right-hand page the reader has not read
src/components/book/PdfBookReader.tsx:351 fix:small conf:high prior:lead:READER-06; AUDIT-2 BOOK-3 covered only the last [blank | endpaper] spread; that fix holds (it now lands on the closing leaf and no stale skip fires)
EVIDENCE: page-flip's current page index is the first leaf of its spread in its own left-to-right order, which after the reversal is the spread's LEFT (second-read) leaf; on an orientation change page-flip re-shows that index (PageFlip.updateOrientation -> update() -> pages.show()), and the reader's `changeOrientation` handler only records the orientation: `flip.on('changeOrientation', (event) => place(event.data === 'portrait'))`. Measured on the export (scratch t5.mjs, 1024x768 -> 768x1024): spread «3–4 / 5» becomes «4 / 5» (page 3 never shown); spread [5 | end] becomes the closing leaf «هنا تنتهي الصفحات المتاحة للقراءة.» with label «نهاية الصفحات المتاحة» (page 5 never shown), screenshot t5-1-portrait.png.
IMPACT: A tablet reader who rotates to portrait, or a desktop reader who narrows the window below 800px, silently skips the right-hand page, which in an Arabic book is the one read first; on the last spread they are told the preview has ended while its last page is unread.
FIX: In place(), when the book turns from two pages to one, move to the spread's right-hand leaf: take `visibleLeaves(indexRef.current, total, false)[0]`, step forward past empty leaves, and `flip.turnToPage(toFlip(target, total))` on the next frame (no animation).

### READER-04 [low] When the first attempt to open the book fails, keyboard focus is left on <body>; only a retry's failure moves it to «إعادة المحاولة»
src/components/book/PdfBookReader.tsx:184 fix:trivial conf:high prior:lead:READER-05; AUDIT-2 FIX-public-8 (second failed retry) and AUDIT-1 S15.3 (retry path) fixed only attempt > 0
EVIDENCE: `if (attempt > 0 && !loaded) (failed ? retryRef : statusRef).current?.focus()` skips attempt 0. The pressed «افتح الكتاب» lives in BookPreview, which is replaced by PdfBookReader's loading tree, so focus already fell to <body>; on success the book effect focuses the host, on a first failure nothing does. Runtime (t10.mjs, PDF request aborted, 1440): after «تعذّر فتح الصفحات.» appears, document.activeElement is BODY; after pressing «إعادة المحاولة» and failing again it is the BUTTON «إعادة المحاولة» (the AUDIT-2 FIX-public-8 path).
IMPACT: A keyboard or screen-reader visitor whose first open fails (flaky mobile data, a blocked request) hears the alert but their focus is at the top of the document; the next Tab goes to the skip link and they must tab through the header and contents strip to reach «إعادة المحاولة» or the PDF link.
FIX: Focus on every failure, not only after a retry: `if (!loaded) (failed ? retryRef : statusRef).current?.focus()` (keep the dependency list), so the first failure lands on «إعادة المحاولة» and the first load on the status region.

### READER-05 [low] Above a 1125px stage (full screen on a 1440p display, any 4K window) page-flip caps pages at 900px while the CSS boards and the cover slide assume the stage's size: oversized boards, off-centre closed book
src/components/book/PdfBookReader.tsx:295 fix:trivial conf:high prior:lead:READER-12
EVIDENCE: page-flip is built with `maxWidth: 900` (page width), but reader.module.css sizes the boards, the focus ring and the closed slide from `--page-w: calc(var(--stage-h) * 0.8)` with `--stage-h: min(78svh, 62.5cqi)` and, in full screen, `min(calc(100svh - 190px), 62.5cqi)` (no upper bound). Measured (t11.mjs): 2560x1440 full screen: CSS page-w 1000 vs page-flip leaf width 900, `.case` 2024px wide around an 1800px spread and 1274px tall around 1125px pages; closed on the cover its centre is at 1330 while the stage centre is 1280. 3840x2160 window: page-w 1348 vs 900, case 2720 wide, cover centre 2258 vs 1920. Screenshots t11-2560-fullscreen.png (a thick saffron frame around the pages) and t11-2560-fullscreen-cover.png (cover pushed right of the centred controls).
IMPACT: A desktop reader who presses «ملء الشاشة» on a common 27-inch 1440p screen (or reads on a 4K screen at 100%) sees the hardcover drawn as a wide saffron frame with the pages floating inside it, and the closed book standing off-centre; the physical-book design breaks exactly in the mode meant to show it best.
FIX: Bound the stage to what page-flip can draw: add 1125px to each --stage-h min() in reader.module.css (`min(78svh, 62.5cqi, 1125px)`, and the same third term in the full-screen and narrow variants), or raise page-flip's maxWidth so it never undercuts the stage; the CSS cap also keeps canvas memory bounded.

### READER-07 [low] PageSurface.draw has no guard for a newer draw that starts while it awaits getPage or getTextContent, so two draws can both finish and stack two text layers (or two renders) in one leaf
src/components/book/pdf.ts:180 fix:small conf:medium
EVIDENCE: draw() cancels only what is stored at entry (`this.cancel()` cancels this.task and this.layer), then awaits `doc.getPage()`, the render, and `page.getTextContent()` before `this.layer = new pdfjs.TextLayer(...)`. A second draw (resize, rotation) that starts while the first awaits getTextContent finds task and layer null, so nothing is cancelled; both continue, and the first's layer can render into this.text around the second's `this.text.replaceChildren()`. A draw that starts during the getPage await likewise leaves two render tasks on one canvas (only the last is kept in this.task). Observed once on the export (t17.mjs: open at 1440, then 12 viewport changes 1400..1000..1200 at 12 ms intervals right after opening): page 2's `.textLayer span` count went from 32 to 64; not reproduced in 13 further runs (t18-t20), so it needs a resize during the leaf's first draw.
IMPACT: Rare: a reader who resizes or rotates while the book is opening can end up with every line of a page duplicated in its (invisible) text layer, so find-in-page reports each hit twice and a select-all copy doubles the text; in the worse case a stale render can mark the canvas. Screen readers are unaffected (the layer is aria-hidden).
FIX: Give PageSurface a draw generation: `const gen = ++this.gen` at the top of draw(), bail out after each await when `gen !== this.gen`, and bump it in cancel()/clear(); cancel the previous layer right before creating the new one.

### READER-08 [low] «التالية» and «السابقة» disable themselves while focused at the back and front covers, dropping keyboard focus to <body>
src/components/book/PdfBookReader.tsx:643 fix:trivial conf:high
EVIDENCE: `<button ... onClick={() => turn(1)} disabled={lastShown === total - 1}>` (and `disabled={(shown[0] ?? 0) === 0}` on «السابقة», line 632). Runtime (t21.mjs, 1440, keyboard only): Enter on a focused «التالية» five times goes 2/5, 3–4/5, 5/5, «نهاية الصفحات المتاحة», back cover; at the back cover the button becomes disabled and document.activeElement is BODY. «السابقة» pressed at «الإهداء» reaches «الغلاف» and focus is BODY as well.
IMPACT: A keyboard or screen-reader reader who pages through with the buttons loses focus at the last (and first) step; screen readers announce nothing useful and may jump the virtual cursor to the top of the page. Sighted keyboard users in Chrome keep a sequential starting point, so the harm is mostly for assistive technology.
FIX: Keep the buttons focusable at the ends: use `aria-disabled` instead of `disabled` (turn() already refuses a step past either end) and style `.control[aria-disabled='true']` like `:disabled`; or move focus to the book host when a focused button becomes disabled.

### READER-09 [low] A stalled preview download leaves the reader on «جارٍ فتح الكتاب…» forever, every control disabled and no PDF link or retry
src/components/book/PdfBookReader.tsx:168 fix:small conf:high
EVIDENCE: openPreview(url) has no timeout and the loading branch renders only `<Opening onOpen={() => {}} loading />` (all buttons disabled, no link); the failure UI with «إعادة المحاولة» and «فتح الصفحات ملفاً (PDF)» appears only if the promise rejects. Runtime (t23.mjs, 390x844, the PDF request held unanswered): after 45 s the status is still «جارٍ فتح الكتاب», buttons «جارٍ فتح الكتاب… (disabled)», «الإهداء (disabled)», «المقدمة (disabled)», «من فصل «صورة الروضة» (disabled)», no links, focus on BODY (screenshot t23-stalled-45s.png). Opening needs about 780 KB brotli (pdf.js 107 KB, worker 307 KB, PDF 341 KB), so on a weak mobile link a long wait is normal and a stall is indistinguishable from it.
IMPACT: A visitor on weak or dropping mobile data who opens the book can wait indefinitely with nothing to press; the only way out is reloading the page, which loses their place in /book.
FIX: Race openPreview against a timer (for example 20 s) that destroys the loading task and shows the existing failure state (retry plus the PDF link), or render the «فتح الصفحات ملفاً (PDF)» link under the loading button from the start.

### READER-11 [low] An arrow key pressed while «النسخ ←» on the closing leaf has focus turns that leaf away and drops focus to <body>
src/components/book/PdfBookReader.tsx:490 fix:trivial conf:high prior:lead:READER-04
EVIDENCE: onKeyDown on the group turns the book whatever descendant has focus (`const key = bookKey(event); ... actions[key.action]()`); page-flip then sets display:none on the hidden leaf that holds the focused link. t26.mjs at 1440: End, Tab -> focus `A النسخ`; ArrowLeft -> label «نهاية الصفحات المتاحة» (the [blank | endpaper] spread) and document.activeElement is BODY.
IMPACT: A keyboard or screen-reader user who tabs onto the end-of-preview call to action and keeps turning with the arrows loses focus to the top of the document; the next arrow press does nothing and the next Tab restarts from the skip link.
FIX: In onKeyDown, when the event target is not the host, move focus to the host before turning (`if (event.target !== event.currentTarget) hostRef.current?.focus({ preventScroll: true })`).

### READER-13 [low] In full screen the reading view's IntersectionObserver gets no look-ahead, so each page shows as blank paper until it scrolls in
src/components/book/StaticPdfReader.tsx:45 fix:small conf:medium prior:lead:READER-08
EVIDENCE: `new IntersectionObserver(..., { rootMargin: '100% 0px' })` uses the viewport as root; in full screen the sheets scroll inside `.reader:fullscreen` (overflow-y: auto), whose clip the root margin does not widen. t26.mjs (full screen, reading view, 1440x900): only page 3, the one on screen, holds a drawn canvas (`drawn: ["3"]`); page 4 just below the fold is not drawn. Out of full screen the same view draws a viewport ahead.
IMPACT: Reading in full screen, every page arrives as plain paper and fills in as it renders, most visibly on a slow phone or tablet.
FIX: Pass the scroll container as the root when it scrolls (`root: document.fullscreenElement ?? null`, re-created on fullscreenchange), keeping rootMargin '100% 0px'.

### READER-14 [low] The e2e guard for 'closed, it loads nothing' cannot see the pdf.js chunk, so an eager pdf.js import would pass
tests/e2e/reader.spec.ts:103 fix:small conf:high prior:lead:READER-10
EVIDENCE: The assertion filters request paths with the regex `.pdf$ | pdf.worker | page-flip` (dots escaped) and expects none. It matches only the PDF, the worker file and a URL containing 'page-flip'. In the export the pdf.js library is /_next/static/chunks/11ph49ml3gu1-.js (433 KB) and page-flip /_next/static/chunks/3y7oy_tt71u1t.js (t1.mjs open requests); neither name matches. A static import of pdfjs-dist into ClosedBook/BookPreview would download pdf.js on every /book visit without requesting the worker (only getDocument starts it), and this assertion would stay green. Today the invariant holds (t1.mjs: no pdf.js, worker, page-flip or PDF request before the click).
IMPACT: D10's lazy load (pdf.js never in the first load of /book) has no effective regression test; a refactor could add ~107 KB brotli of pdf.js (plus page-flip) to every visit unnoticed.
FIX: Assert on content instead of names: before the click, read the loaded script URLs from `performance.getEntriesByType('resource')`, fetch them, and expect none to contain `GlobalWorkerOptions` or `stf__parent`.

### READER-15 [low] docs/book-preview.md says a key pressed during a turn is ignored; the reader queues the last move and makes it after the turn
docs/book-preview.md:64 fix:trivial conf:high prior:lead:READER-09
EVIDENCE: Line 64: «A key pressed while a page is still turning is ignored». PdfBookReader.tsx stores the last move in pendingRef while page-flip is 'flipping' (lines 211-213, 231-233) and replays it at 'read' (372-381), as its own comment at 133-135 says. t4.mjs: two ArrowLeft presses 80 ms apart from «الإهداء 1 / 5» end on «3–4 / 5».
IMPACT: A maintainer or test writer is told the opposite of what the code does; a test built from the doc fails, or the queue (the AUDIT-2 BOOK-1 fix) gets 'fixed' away.
FIX: Reword: a key pressed while a page is turning is kept (the last one) and made when the turn ends.

### READER-16 [low] The phone reading view never redraws for pinch zoom: the zoom its own hint asks for magnifies a 2x bitmap of 6.9px text
src/components/book/StaticPdfReader.tsx:32 fix:small conf:medium prior:lead:READER-11
EVIDENCE: drawNear() draws each sheet at host.clientWidth with draw()'s default maxDpr 2 (pdf.ts:149-151: `const dpr = Math.min(window.devicePixelRatio || 1, maxDpr)`); only the IntersectionObserver and a ResizeObserver call it, and neither fires on pinch zoom (nothing in src/components/book reads visualViewport). Measured at 360x780 DPR 3 (t8.mjs, t9b.mjs, t9c.mjs): the page text is 6.86 CSS px in both the book and the reading view (the sheet is 320 px wide on a phone); the page-1 canvas is 640x800 for the 320 px sheet before and after a 2.5x page-scale zoom (visualViewport.scale 2.5), so each canvas pixel covers 3.75 screen pixels; t9c-zoom25.png shows the zoomed Arabic soft next to the crisp HTML text. The hint under the tools reads «كبّر الصفحة بإصبعين لقراءة أوضح.»
IMPACT: On a phone the only way to read Anas's pages is to pinch, as the page itself instructs, and the result is soft, upscaled Arabic (dots and harakat blur) on the device most visitors use.
FIX: Redraw the sheets in view at `devicePixelRatio * visualViewport.scale` (capped, e.g. at 4) on visualViewport 'resize' (debounced), and clear back to the base resolution when the zoom returns to 1; the surfaces already cancel stale renders.

### READER-17 [low] The preview's 'no email may remain' check scans the raw PDF bytes, so it cannot see an address inside the compressed page or font streams
scripts/prepare-preview.py:118 fix:trivial conf:high
EVIDENCE: verify() runs `assert EMAIL.search(data) is None` on the written file's bytes, and tests/e2e/reader.spec.ts:96 checks `bytes.includes(Buffer.from('@gmail'))` the same way; but every stream in the output is Flate-compressed (22 /FlateDecode in out/book/khous-preview.pdf; each page's /Contents is /FlateDecode), and `zlib.compress(b'BT (anas@example.com) Tj ET')` contains no '@example'. Today the decoded streams hold no address (inspect_pdf.py decoded all 22 streams: emails set()), so the shipped file is clean; the check simply cannot fail for text inside a page or a font.
IMPACT: The plan treats Anas's email (carried by his Word exports) as something the public preview must never contain; if a future fragment carries an address in its page text or font data, the script and the test still pass and the address is published.
FIX: Run the EMAIL check (and the forbidden-key check) on every decoded stream and on each page's extracted text, e.g. iterate the reader's objects and test `obj.get_data()` for StreamObjects, as scratch inspect_pdf.py does.

### SOON-05 [low] In maintenance, /robots.txt (and /sitemap.xml) answer with the 503 maintenance page, which Google says not to do
PLANS/SITE-STATE-CONTRACT.md:43 fix:trivial conf:high prior:none (lead:SOON-02 mentions robots.txt only as a script run)
EVIDENCE: The contract's pass-through list for soon and maintenance (line 43) names /admin, /admin.txt, /admin/*, /checkout/return, /orders, /notify/confirm, /notify/unsubscribe, /book/* and the static files, not /robots.txt or /sitemap.xml, so in maintenance they get the 503 design. Today's Worker does the same: site/ has no robots.txt, so it is an unknown path and worker.js lines 21-33 answer it with a design (live 2026-10-07: `curl -sS -D - -o /dev/null https://anas.studio/robots.txt` -> `HTTP/1.1 200 OK`, `Content-Type: text/html`, `Set-Cookie: soon=a...`; 503 when MODE is maintenance). Google, https://developers.google.com/search/docs/crawling-indexing/pause-online-business : "Don't block your robots.txt file with a 503 HTTP response status code."; https://developers.google.com/search/docs/crawling-indexing/robots/robots_txt : for server errors "For the first 12 hours, Google stops crawling the site but keeps trying to fetch the robots.txt file."
IMPACT: After the launch, every «صيانة» period serves robots.txt with 503: Google stops crawling the whole site for 12 hours after each one and then works from its last good copy, and the sitemap is unreachable for the duration. Before the launch it costs nothing (the page is noindex).
FIX: Add /robots.txt and /sitemap.xml to the gate's pass-through list and to its node --test cases; in the soon Worker, a static site/robots.txt (served as a file, which also spends no script run).

### SOON-06 [low] «صيانة» does not hold through a database outage: a fresh isolate has no last answer and falls back to MODE, which is `open` after the launch
PLANS/SITE-STATE-CONTRACT.md:43 fix:small conf:medium prior:none
EVIDENCE: Line 43: the state is 'remembered for 30 seconds per isolate'; 'A failed read keeps the last answer; with no answer yet it uses `MODE`, which is `soon` before the launch and `open` from the launch'. Cloudflare, https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/reference/how-workers-works/ : 'isolates are not necessarily long-lived. An isolate may be spun down and evicted for a number of reasons... it is generally advised that you not store mutable state in your global scope unless you have accounted for this contingency.' Each data center runs its own isolates, so the 'last answer' exists only in isolates that read the row before it became unreachable.
IMPACT: When the owner puts the site in «صيانة» because the database is failing (one of the cases D46 names), every isolate that starts after the reads begin failing (a new data center, an evicted isolate) has no last answer and serves the launched site, which is broken without its database; visitors get a mix of the maintenance page and a failing site. Only a deploy of MODE = maintenance holds, and the owner cannot deploy.
FIX: Give the fallback a second level before MODE: on every good read also `caches.default.put` a small response holding the word (long max-age), and on a failed read with no in-memory answer read it back from the data center's cache; fall back to MODE only when neither exists. Say in the admin's «صيانة» line that a database outage needs the deploy switch.

### SOON-07 [low] The admin shows the stored word, but the deploy override and the fallback can make the domain show another state, and the availability sweep follows the stored word too
PLANS/SITE-STATE-CONTRACT.md:62 fix:small conf:high prior:related: FIX-A2-04 (the sweep ignores other closures)
EVIDENCE: Line 62: 'Truthful states: the form shows the state it read back'; line 37: 'The form reads `mode, version` from the table itself'. Line 43: '`MODE = "maintenance"` set by a deploy wins over the stored state' and MODE also answers when reads fail. Line 64: the availability sweep's new guard is '(select mode from public.site_state) = 'open'', the stored word. Nothing in the contract reports the effective state back to the admin.
IMPACT: During a migration or restore done with `deploy.cjs --mode=maintenance` (the contract's own switch for that), and afterwards if nobody redeploys the normal mode, the admin says «الموقع الآن: مفتوح» while every visitor gets the 503 page; the owner's buttons change nothing he can see and nothing tells him why. In the same window the sweep still mails subscribers a /store/<slug> link the gate answers with 503, spending their one notice per revision.
FIX: Let the gate add one header to its answers, for example `X-Site-State: maintenance; source=deploy|stored|fallback`; the form reads it with a same-origin HEAD of `/` and shows a line when it differs from the stored word; the restore and migration runbooks end with redeploying the normal MODE. (The sweep guard can stay on the stored word if the runbook also says to set «صيانة» in the admin during such work.)

### SOON-08 [low] The soon page rides on the Workers Free daily allowance: past 100,000 script runs in a UTC day, anas.studio/ shows Cloudflare error 1027 until midnight UTC
artifacts/soon/wrangler.toml:24 OWNER fix:trivial conf:high prior:lead:SOON-02; related PLAN-GAPS-06 (cost sheet and E07 say no Workers)
EVIDENCE: Every address that is not a file runs the script: live 2026-10-07, `/`, `/no-such-page`, `/robots.txt` and `/_headers` all answer with `Set-Cookie: soon=...` from worker.js line 35, so each view of / and each bot or scanner path costs one run. The Worker is reachable only through Custom Domains (wrangler.toml lines 24-32, `custom_domain = true`), where it is the origin. https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/platform/limits/ : 'Accounts on the Workers Free plan have a daily request limit of 100,000 requests, resetting at midnight UTC. When a Worker exceeds this limit, Cloudflare returns Error 1027.'; fail open or closed is set per route, and a Custom Domain has no origin behind it to fail open to. Pages Functions count against the same allowance (https://developers.cloudflare.com/pages/functions/pricing/).
IMPACT: If Anas shares the link with his audience and a day's views plus bot traffic pass 100,000 script runs (counted per account, with any other Worker or Pages Function there), visitors to anas.studio get Cloudflare's error page instead of the soon page until 03:00 Riyadh time. Nothing records the ceiling.
FIX: Record the ceiling in artifacts/soon/README.md and in P11's traffic check; serve a static site/robots.txt so crawlers spend no run; the owner decides whether a launch period on Workers Paid is worth it (E07).

### SOON-09 [low] deploy.cjs takes CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN and CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID from the shell when they are set there, not from .env, so a shell set up for another account deploys there
artifacts/soon/deploy.cjs:28 fix:trivial conf:high prior:related: MAINT-1 review fix 'the deploy script passes wrangler nothing else from .env' (holds)
EVIDENCE: Lines 14-16 only check that the two names exist in process.env; line 28 `const env = { ...process.env }` and lines 29-32 delete only the other names .env defines, so the two kept values are whatever process.env holds. Node's --env-file does not override a variable the shell already has: `AUDIT_FOO=from-shell node --env-file=envtest.env -e "console.log(process.env.AUDIT_FOO)"` with `AUDIT_FOO=from-file` in the file prints `from-shell` (node v24.19.0, run in SCRATCH). README line 57: 'The deploy goes to Anas's Cloudflare account with CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN and CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID from .env'.
IMPACT: In a terminal that already exports CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN or CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID for another account (the owner's own Cloudflare tooling), `deploy.cjs --mode=maintenance` uploads a stray anas-studio-soon Worker to that account and fails at the custom-domain step, so the maintenance switch does not happen when it is needed and the operator has to work out why.
FIX: Read the two values from the parsed .env and set them on `env` explicitly (`const file = parseEnv(...); env.CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN = file.CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN; env.CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID = file.CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`), refusing to run when .env lacks either.

### STORE-UI-03 [low] The return, cart and checkout pages bake a loading line into the static HTML that nothing ends without JavaScript (the rule R10A-5 applied to /orders only)
src/components/store/PaymentReturn.tsx:138 fix:small conf:high prior:lead:STORE-UI-01; P08 round 10a R10A-5 (fixed for /orders only)
